\mathbbm{E}[e^{(\alpha-\gamma)(h,\widehat{\theta}_{\varepsilon})}]=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\varepsilon\mathrm{CR}(\eta_{1},0)}{2}}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}(\alpha-\gamma)^{2}}
.
Since the law of
\widetilde{h}+c
under
e^{(\alpha+\gamma-2Q)c}dc\mathbbm{P}_{\mathbbm{C}}(dh)
is
\mathrm{LF}_{\mathbbm{C}}^{(\alpha,0),(\gamma,1)}
, we get (
8.7
).

Combining (
8.5
), (
8.6
) and (
8.7
), and collecting the prefactors via

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\mathrm{CR}(\eta_{1},0)}{2}}}\right)^{(\alpha-\gamma)Q}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\varepsilon\mathrm{CR}(\eta_{1},0)}{2}}}\right)^{-(\alpha-\gamma)\gamma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\varepsilon\mathrm{CR}(\eta_{1},0)}{2}}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}(\alpha-\gamma)^{2}}=\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}(\alpha^{2}-\gamma^{2})}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\mathrm{CR}(\eta_{1},0)}{2}}}\right)^{-\frac{\alpha^{2}}{2}+Q\alpha-2},

we conclude the proof of Lemma
8.9
.
∎

Suppose
\eta
is a simple curve in
\mathcal{C}
separating
\pm\infty
with two marked points
p^{-},p^{+}\in\eta
. Let
D^{\pm}_{\eta}
be the connected components of
\mathcal{C}\backslash\eta
containing
\pm\infty
, and let
\psi^{\pm}_{\eta}:\mathbbm{H}\to D^{\pm}_{\eta}
be the conformal maps sending
(i,0)
to
(\pm\infty,p^{\pm})
.
We need the following lemma, which is essentially the variant of Lemma
8.9
on
\mathcal{C}
where
\gamma
-insertions at
\pm\infty
are changed to
\alpha
-insertions.

Lemma 8.10
.

Let
\eta
be a simple curve in
\mathcal{C}
that separates
\pm\infty
with two marked points
p^{-},p^{+}\in\eta
.
For
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
, let
\mathcal{C}_{\eta,p^{\pm},\varepsilon}=\mathcal{C}\setminus(\psi^{-}_{\eta}(B_{\varepsilon}(i))\cup\psi^{+}_{\eta}(B_{\varepsilon}(i)))
.
For
\phi
sampled from
\mathrm{LF}_{\mathcal{C}}^{(\gamma,\pm\infty)}
, let
X^{\pm}=\phi\circ\psi^{\pm}_{\eta}+Q\log|(\psi^{\pm}_{\eta})^{\prime}|
.
Then for a fixed
\alpha\in(\frac{\gamma}{2},Q)
and
for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
and any nonnegative measurable function
f
of
\phi|_{\mathcal{C}_{\eta,p^{\pm},\varepsilon}}
we have

\displaystyle\int f(\phi|_{\mathcal{C}_{\eta,p^{\pm},\varepsilon}})\times\varepsilon^{\alpha^{2}-\gamma^{2}}e^{(\alpha-\gamma)(X^{-}_{\varepsilon}(i)+X^{+}_{\varepsilon}(i))}\,d\mathrm{LF}_{\mathcal{C}}^{(\gamma,\pm\infty)}

\displaystyle=\int f(\phi|_{\mathcal{C}_{\eta,p^{\pm},\varepsilon}})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{4}\mathrm{CR}(\exp(\eta),0)\mathrm{CR}(\exp(-\eta),0)}}\right)^{-\frac{\alpha^{2}}{2}+Q\alpha-2}\,d\mathrm{LF}_{\mathcal{C}}^{(\alpha,\pm\infty)}.

Proof.

Let
g:\mathbbm{C}\to\mathbbm{C}
be given by
g(z)=\frac{z}{z-1}
and let
G:\mathcal{C}\to\mathbbm{C}
be given by
G=g\circ\exp
. By
[
AHS24
, Lemma 2.13]
, if
\phi
is sampled from
\mathrm{LF}_{\mathcal{C}}^{(\gamma,\pm\infty)}
then
\hat{\phi}:=\phi\circ G^{-1}+Q\log|(G^{-1})^{\prime}|
has law
\mathrm{LF}_{\mathbbm{C}}^{(\gamma,0),(\gamma,+1)}
, and the same is true when
\gamma
is replaced by
\alpha
. Let
(\hat{\eta},\hat{p}^{-},\hat{p}^{+})=(G(\eta),G(p^{-}),G(p^{+}))
. Since
g^{\prime}(0)=-1
and
\frac{d}{dz}(g(\frac{1}{z}))|_{z=0}=1
, we see that
\mathrm{CR}(\exp(\eta),0)=\mathrm{CR}(\hat{\eta},0)
and
\mathrm{CR}(\exp(-\eta),0)=\mathrm{CR}(\hat{\eta},+1)
. Let
\mathbbm{C}_{\hat{\eta},\hat{p}^{\pm},\varepsilon}:=\mathbbm{C}\backslash(G(\psi^{-}(B_{\varepsilon}(i)))\cup G(\psi^{+}(B_{\varepsilon}(i))))
. Then Lemma
8.10
is equivalent to the following:
for any nonnegative measurable function
\hat{f}
of
\hat{\phi}
that depends only on
\hat{\phi}|_{\mathbbm{C}_{\hat{\eta},\hat{p}^{\pm},\varepsilon}}
, we have

\displaystyle\int\hat{f}(\hat{\phi})\times\varepsilon^{\alpha^{2}-\gamma^{2}}e^{(\alpha-\gamma)(X^{-}_{\varepsilon}(i)+X^{+}_{\varepsilon}(i))}\,d\mathrm{LF}_{\mathbbm{C}}^{(\gamma,0),(\gamma,+1)}

\displaystyle=\int\hat{f}(\hat{\phi})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{4}\mathrm{CR}(\hat{\eta},0)\mathrm{CR}(\hat{\eta},+1)}}\right)^{-\frac{\alpha^{2}}{2}+Q\alpha-2}d\mathrm{LF}_{\mathbbm{C}}^{(\alpha,0),(\alpha,+1)}.

(8.8)

Now we apply Lemma
8.9
,we get

\displaystyle\int\hat{f}(\hat{\phi})\times\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}(\alpha^{2}-\gamma^{2})}e^{(\alpha-\gamma)X^{-}_{\varepsilon}(i)}\,d\mathrm{LF}_{\mathbbm{C}}^{(\gamma,0),(\gamma,+1)}=\int\hat{f}(\hat{\phi})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{CR}(\hat{\eta},0)}}\right)^{-\frac{\alpha^{2}}{2}+Q\alpha-2}d\mathrm{LF}_{\mathbbm{C}}^{(\alpha,0),(\gamma,+1)}.

Applying the argument of Lemma
8.9
again to change the insertion at
+1
, we get (
8.8
).
∎

For a curve
\eta
in
\mathcal{C}
that separates
\pm\infty
, we let
\mathrm{Harm}_{-\infty,\eta}
(resp.
\mathrm{Harm}_{+\infty,\eta}
) be the harmonic measure on
\eta
viewed from
-\infty
(resp.,
+\infty
).

Lemma 8.11
.

There is a constant
C=C(\gamma)
such that the following holds.
Suppose
\alpha\in(\frac{\gamma}{2},Q)
.
Sample
(\phi,\eta,p^{-},p^{+})
from the measure

C\cdot\mathrm{LF}_{\mathcal{C}}^{(\alpha,\pm\infty)}(d\phi)\,\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}(d\eta)\,\mathrm{Harm}_{-\infty,\eta}(dp^{-})\,\mathrm{Harm}_{+\infty,\eta}(dp^{+}).

Let
X_{\pm}=\phi\circ\psi_{\eta}^{\pm}+Q\log|(\psi_{\eta}^{\pm})^{\prime}|
. Let
\tau
be the quantum length of the clockwise arc from
p^{-}
to
p^{+}
in
D^{+}_{\eta}
. Then the law of
(X^{-},X^{+},\tau)
is

\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{LF}_{\mathbbm{H}}^{(\alpha,i)}(\ell)\times\mathrm{LF}_{\mathbbm{H}}^{(\alpha,i)}(\ell)\times[1_{\tau\in(0,\ell)}d\tau]\,d\ell.

Proof.

We first prove the case
\alpha=\gamma
, namely

C\cdot\mathrm{LF}_{\mathcal{C}}^{(\gamma,\pm\infty)}(d\phi)\,\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}(d\eta)\,\mathrm{Harm}_{-\infty,\eta}(dp^{-})\,\mathrm{Harm}_{+\infty,\eta}(dp^{+})=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{LF}_{\mathbbm{H}}^{(\gamma,i)}(\ell)\times\mathrm{LF}_{\mathbbm{H}}^{(\gamma,i)}(\ell)\times[1_{\tau\in(0,\ell)}d\tau]\,d\ell,

(8.9)

where with abuse of notation we view the left-hand side as a measure on triples
(X^{-},X^{+},\tau)\in H^{-1}(\mathbbm{H})\times H^{-1}(\mathbbm{H})\times[0,\infty)
. Indeed, (
8.9
) is an immediate consequence of Proposition
6.5
and Lemma
2.21
.

Now, let
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
and
\mathbbm{H}_{\varepsilon}:=\mathbbm{H}\backslash B_{\varepsilon}(i)
.
Let
f
be a nonnegative measurable function of
(X^{-}|_{\mathbbm{H}_{\varepsilon}},X^{+}|_{\mathbbm{H}_{\varepsilon}},\tau)
, then reweighting (
8.9
) gives

\displaystyle C\int f(X^{-}|_{\mathbbm{H}_{\varepsilon}},X^{+}|_{\mathbbm{H}_{\varepsilon}},\tau)\varepsilon^{\alpha^{2}-\gamma^{2}}e^{(\alpha-\gamma)(X^{-}_{\varepsilon}(i)+X^{+}_{\varepsilon}(i))}\mathrm{LF}_{\mathcal{C}}^{(\gamma,\pm\infty)}(d\phi)\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}(d\eta)\mathrm{Harm}_{-\infty,\eta}(dp^{+})\mathrm{Harm}_{-\infty,\eta}(dp^{+})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int f(X^{-}|_{\mathbbm{H}_{\varepsilon}},X^{+}|_{\mathbbm{H}_{\varepsilon}},\tau)\varepsilon^{\alpha^{2}-\gamma^{2}}e^{(\alpha-\gamma)(X^{-}_{\varepsilon}(i)+X^{+}_{\varepsilon}(i))}\ell\mathrm{LF}_{\mathbbm{H}}^{(\gamma,i)}(\ell)\times\mathrm{LF}_{\mathbbm{H}}^{(\gamma,i)}(\ell)\times[1_{\tau\in(0,\ell)}d\tau]}}\right)\,d\ell.

By Lemma
8.10
, the left hand side equals

C\int f(X^{-}|_{\mathbbm{H}_{\varepsilon}},X^{+}|_{\mathbbm{H}_{\varepsilon}},\tau)\mathrm{LF}_{\mathcal{C}}^{(\alpha,\pm\infty)}(d\phi)\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}(d\eta)\mathrm{Harm}_{-\infty,\eta}(dp^{-})\mathrm{Harm}_{+\infty,\eta}(dp^{+}).

By Lemma
8.8
, the right hand side equals

\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int f(X^{-}|_{\mathbbm{H}_{\varepsilon}},X^{+}|_{\mathbbm{H}_{\varepsilon}},\tau)\mathrm{LF}_{\mathbbm{H}}^{(\alpha,i)}(\ell)\times\mathrm{LF}_{\mathbbm{H}}^{(\alpha,i)}(\ell)\times[1_{\tau\in(0,\ell)}d\tau]}}\right)\,d\ell.

Since the above two expressions agree for every
\varepsilon
and
f
, we obtain the result.
∎

Proof of Proposition
8.4
.

In Lemma
8.11
, the law of
(\mathcal{C},\phi,\eta,\pm\infty)/{\sim_{\gamma}}
is
C(Q-\alpha)^{2}\mathcal{M}_{2}^{\mathrm{sph}}(W)\otimes\SLE_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{sep},\alpha}
by (
8.3
), and the joint law of
((\mathbbm{H},X^{-},i)/{\sim_{\gamma}},(\mathbbm{H},X^{+},i)/{\sim_{\gamma}},\tau)
is
\int_{0}^{\infty}\ell\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\mathrm{disk}}(\alpha;\ell)\times\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\mathrm{disk}}(\alpha;\ell)\times[1_{\tau\in(0,\ell)}\ell^{-1}d\tau]\,d\ell
. Since
[1_{\tau\in(0,\ell)}\ell^{-1}d\tau]
corresponds to uniform conformal welding, the law of
(\mathcal{C},\phi,\eta,\pm\infty)/{\sim_{\gamma}}
is
\int_{0}^{\infty}\ell\mathrm{Weld}(\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\mathrm{disk}}(\alpha;\ell),\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\mathrm{disk}}(\alpha;\ell))\,d\ell
.
∎

8.3
The appearance of
\overline{R}(\alpha)
and
|\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}|
: proof of Proposition
8.6

We will prove Proposition
8.6
via a particular embedding of
\mathcal{M}_{2}^{\mathrm{sph}}(W)
.
Let
h
be the field
\hat{h}+\mathbf{c}
in Definition
2.6
so that the law of
(\mathcal{C},h,-\infty,+\infty)/{\sim_{\gamma}}
is
\mathcal{M}_{2}^{\mathrm{sph}}(W)
.
Now we restrict to the event
\{\mu_{h}(\mathcal{C})>1\}
and set
\phi:=h(\cdot-a)
, where
a\in\mathbbm{R}
is such that
\mu_{h}((-\infty,a)\times[0,2\pi])=1
.
Namely, we shift
h
horizontally such that
\mu_{\phi}(\{z\in\mathcal{C}:\Re z\leq 0\})=1
.
Let
M
be the law of
\phi
under this restriction. Then we can represent
\mathcal{M}_{2}^{\mathrm{sph}}(W)\otimes\SLE_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{sep},\alpha}[E_{\delta,\varepsilon}]
in Proposition
8.6
as follows.

Lemma 8.12
.

Given a simple closed curve
\eta
on
\mathcal{C}
separating
\pm\infty
,
let
\mathcal{C}_{\eta}^{+}
(resp.
\mathcal{C}_{\eta}^{-}
) be the connected component of
\mathcal{C}\backslash\eta
containing
+\infty
(resp.
-\infty
).
For
t\in\mathbbm{R}
, let
\eta+t
be the curve on
\mathcal{C}
obtained by shifting
\eta
by
t
.
Now sample
(\phi,\mathbf{t},\eta^{0})
from
M\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}
and set
\eta=\eta^{0}+\mathbf{t}
.
Let

E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}:=\{(\phi,\eta):\varepsilon<\ell_{\phi}(\eta)<\delta\text{ and }\mu_{\phi}(\mathcal{C}_{\eta}^{-})>1\}

(8.10)

where
\ell_{\phi}(\eta)
is the quantum length of
\eta
.
Then
\mathcal{M}_{2}^{\mathrm{sph}}(W)\otimes\SLE_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{sep},\alpha}[E_{\delta,\varepsilon}]=(M\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha})[E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}].

Proof.

Since the measure
\SLE_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{sep},\alpha}
is invariant under translations along the cylinder,
the law of
(\mathcal{C},\phi,\eta,+\infty,-\infty)/{\sim_{\gamma}}
is the restriction of
\mathcal{M}_{2}^{\mathrm{sph}}(W)\otimes\SLE_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{sep},\alpha}
to
the event that the total quantum area is larger than
1
.
Now Lemma
8.12
follows from the definition of
E_{\delta,\varepsilon}
.
∎

The next lemma explains how the reflection coefficient
\overline{R}(\alpha)
shows up in Proposition
8.6
.

Lemma 8.13
.

|M|=\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2}\overline{R}(\alpha)a^{\frac{2}{\gamma}(\alpha-Q)-1}\,da=\frac{\gamma\overline{R}(\alpha)}{4(Q-\alpha)}
.

Proof.

This simply comes from Lemma
2.8
.
∎

By Lemmas
8.10
and
8.13
, Proposition
8.6
is reduced to the following proposition.

Proposition 8.14
.

Let
\alpha\in(\frac{\gamma}{2},Q)
. With an error term
o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1)
satisfying
\lim_{\delta\to 0}\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}|o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1)|=0
, we have

(M\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha})[E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}]=(1+o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1))|M||\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}|\frac{2\log\varepsilon^{-1}}{\gamma(Q-\alpha)}.

(8.11)

Proof of Proposition
8.6
given Proposition
8.14
.

By Lemma
8.12
and
8.13
, we have

(\mathcal{M}_{2}^{\mathrm{sph}}(W)\otimes\SLE_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{sep},\alpha})[E_{\delta,\varepsilon}]=(1+o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1))|M||\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}|\frac{2\log\varepsilon^{-1}}{\gamma(Q-\alpha)}=(1+o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1))\frac{\overline{R}(\alpha)}{2(Q-\alpha)^{2}}|\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}|\log\varepsilon^{-1}.\qed

Figure 9:
Illustration of the proof of Proposition
8.14
. On the left
(\phi,\mathbf{t},\eta^{0})
is sampled from
M\times dt\times\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa}
and
\eta=\eta^{0}+\mathbf{t}
. The curve
\eta^{0}
satisfies
\sup_{z\in\eta_{0}}\Re z=0
and the field
\phi
satisfies
\mu_{\phi}(\{z\in\mathcal{C}:\Re z\leq 0\})=1
. The domain
D^{-}_{\eta}
is colored grey. The event
E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}
is
\{\ell_{\phi}(\eta)\in(\varepsilon,\delta)\textrm{ and }\mu_{\phi}(\mathcal{C}_{\eta}^{-})>1\}
. If
E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}
occurs, we have
\ell_{\phi}(\eta)\approx e^{\frac{\gamma}{2}X_{\bf t}}
. Therefore
\mathbf{t}
is in an interval close to the red one on the right figure, which is approxiately
(\frac{2}{\gamma(Q-\alpha)}\log\delta^{-1},\frac{2}{\gamma(Q-\alpha)}\log\varepsilon^{-1})
. The proofs of Lemmas
8.18
and
8.20
are done by quantifying this statement in two directions.

The high level idea for proving Proposition
8.14
is the following. Suppose
(\phi,\mathbf{t},\eta^{0})
is sampled from
M\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}
.
For
s\geq 0
, let
X_{s}
be the average of
\phi
on
[s,s+2\pi i]/{\sim}
.
For most realizations of
(\phi,\eta^{0})
, the occurrence of
E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}
is equivalent to
the event that
\mathbf{t}
lies in some interval of length
(1+o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1))\frac{2\log\varepsilon^{-1}}{\gamma(Q-\alpha)}
determined by
X
. (See Figure
9
.)
Hence the mass of
E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}
is this length times
|M||\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}|
.
In the rest of this section, we first prove a few properties for
X
in Section
8.3.1

and then prove Proposition
8.14
in Section
8.3.2
.

8.3.1
The field average process

We need the following description of the law of
\phi|_{\mathcal{C}_{+}}
and
(X_{s})_{s\geq 0}
, where
\mathcal{C}_{+}:=\{z\in\mathcal{C}\>:\>\Re z>0\}
is the right half cylinder.

Lemma 8.15
.

Let
\phi
be a sample from
M
.
Conditioned on
\phi|_{\mathcal{C}_{-}}
, the conditional law of
\phi|_{\mathcal{C}_{+}}
is the law of
\phi_{0}+\hat{\mathfrak{h}}-(Q-\alpha)\Re(\cdot)
, where
\phi_{0}
is a zero boundary GFF on
\mathcal{C}_{+}
, and
\hat{\mathfrak{h}}
is a harmonic function determined by
\phi|_{\mathcal{C}_{-}}
whose average on the circle
[s,s+2\pi i]/{\sim}
does not depend on
s
.
Moreover, let
X_{s}
be the average of
\phi
on
[s,s+2\pi i]/{\sim}
. Conditioned on
X_{0}
, the conditional law of
(X_{s}-X_{0})_{s\geq 0}
is the law of
(B_{s}-(Q-\alpha)s)_{s\geq 0}
where
B_{s}
is a Brownian motion.

Proof.

The first statement is the sphere analog of
[
AG21
, Lemmas 2.10 and 2.11]
based on the domain Markov property of Gaussian free field.
The proof is identical so we omit it. The second statement on
(X_{s})_{s\geq 0}
follows from the first statement.
∎

Proposition
8.14
essentially follows from the fact that the field average process
(X_{s})_{s\geq 0}
looks like a line of slope
-(Q-\alpha)
. We now introduce two random times to quantify this. For
y>0
, let

\sigma_{y}=\inf\{s>0\>:\>X_{s}<\frac{2}{\gamma}\log y\}\quad\textrm{and}\quad\tau_{y}=\sup\{s>0\>:\>X_{s}>\frac{2}{\gamma}\log y\}.

(8.12)

Lemma 8.16
.

M
-a.e. the field
\phi
satisfies
\lim_{y\to 0}\frac{\sigma_{y}}{\log y^{-1}}=\lim_{y\to 0}\frac{\tau_{y}}{\log y^{-1}}=\frac{2}{\gamma(Q-\alpha)}
.

Proof.

Given Lemma
8.15
, this is a straightforward fact about drifted Brownian motion.
∎

The proof of the upper bound in Proposition
8.14
will rely on the following lemma.

Lemma 8.17
.

Sample
(\phi,\mathbf{t},\eta^{0})
from
M\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}
and set
\eta=\eta^{0}+\mathbf{t}
. Let
\mathfrak{l}=\ell_{\phi}(\eta)
, which is the boundary length of the quantum surface
(\mathcal{C}_{\eta}^{-},\phi)/{\sim_{\gamma}}
.
Fix
\zeta\in(0,1)
.
Then there exists a constant
C>0
and a function
\mathrm{err}(\ell)
such that
\lim_{\ell\downarrow 0}\mathrm{err}(\ell)=0
, and conditioned on

(\mathcal{C}_{\eta}^{-},\phi)/{\sim_{\gamma}}
,
the conditional probability of
\{\mathbf{t}\in(\sigma_{{\mathfrak{l}}^{1-\zeta}}-C,\tau_{{\mathfrak{l}}^{1+\zeta}})\}
is at least
1-\mathrm{err}(\mathfrak{l})
.

Proof.

We first introduce
C
and
\mathrm{err},
and then show that they satisfy Lemma
8.17
.
Recall that
\eta^{0}
is a loop in
\mathcal{C}
separating
\pm\infty
and with
\sup_{z\in\eta^{0}}\Re z=0
.
Let
\mathcal{C}_{\eta^{0}}^{+}
be the connected component of
\mathcal{C}\backslash\eta^{0}
containing
+\infty
.
Let
f:\mathcal{C}_{+}\to\mathcal{C}_{\eta^{0}}^{+}
be the unique conformal map such that
f(+\infty)=+\infty
and
\lim_{z\to+\infty}(f(z)-z)\in\mathbbm{R}
.
By standard conformal distortion estimates (e.g.
[
MS19
, Lemma 2.4]
), for
C=e^{10}

we have

|f(z)-z|<\frac{C}{3}\quad\textrm{and}\quad|f^{\prime\prime}(z)|<\frac{1}{2}<|f^{\prime}(z)|<2\quad\textrm{for }\Re z>\frac{C}{3}.

(8.13)

Let
g
be a fixed smooth function on
\mathcal{C}
supported on
\{\Re z\in(C-1,C)\}
such that
\int g(z)\,dz=1
and
g
is invariant under rotations against the axis of
\mathcal{C}
. Let
S
be the collection of smooth functions
\xi
that are supported on
\{\Re z\in[\frac{2}{3}C-1,\frac{4}{3}C]\}\subset\mathcal{C}_{+}
and satisfy
\|\xi\|_{\infty}\leq 4\|g\|_{\infty}
and
\|\nabla\xi\|_{\infty}\leq 8(\|g\|_{\infty}+\|\nabla g\|_{\infty})
. Let

\mathrm{err}(\ell):=\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\mathrm{disk}}(\alpha;1)^{\#}\Big[\sup_{\xi\in S}|(h,\xi)|+Q\log 2\geq-\frac{2\zeta}{\gamma}\log\ell\Big]

(8.14)

where
(\mathcal{C}_{+},h,+\infty)
is an embedding of a sample from
\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\mathrm{disk}}(\alpha;1)^{\#}
, the probablity measure proportional to
\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\mathrm{disk}}(\alpha;1)
. Since the space
S
is invariant under rotations against the axis of
\mathcal{C}
, the probability in (
8.14
) does not depend on the choice of the embedding. Moreover, since
h
is a.s. in the Sobolev space of index
-1
, we see that

\sup_{\xi\in S}|(h,\xi)|<\infty
a.s. hence
\lim_{\ell\to 0}\mathrm{err}(\ell)=0
.

We now show that
C
and
\mathrm{err}
satisfy Lemma
8.17
. Set
\phi^{0}(\cdot)=\phi(\cdot+\mathbf{t})
and
\hat{\phi}=\phi^{0}\circ f+Q\log|f^{\prime}|-\frac{2}{\gamma}\log\mathfrak{l}
.
Then
(\mathcal{C}_{\eta}^{+},\phi,+\infty)/{\sim_{\gamma}}=(D_{\eta_{0}}^{+},\phi^{0},+\infty)/{\sim_{\gamma}}
hence
(\mathcal{C}_{+},\hat{\phi},+\infty)/{\sim_{\gamma}}=(\mathcal{C}_{\eta}^{+},\phi-\frac{2}{\gamma}\log\mathfrak{l},+\infty)/{\sim_{\gamma}}
.
Moreover,

(\phi^{0},g)-\frac{2}{\gamma}\log\mathfrak{l}=(\hat{\phi}\circ f^{-1}+Q\log|(f^{-1})^{\prime}|,g)=(\hat{\phi},|f^{\prime}|^{2}g\circ f)+(Q\log|(f^{-1})^{\prime}|,g).

(8.15)

By (
8.13
) and the definition of
S
, we have
|f^{\prime}|^{2}g\circ f\in S
. Then by (
8.13
) and (
8.15
), we have

\big|(\phi^{0},g)-\frac{2}{\gamma}\log\mathfrak{l}\big|\leq|(\hat{\phi},|f^{\prime}|^{2}g\circ f)|+|(Q\log|(f^{-1})^{\prime}|,g)|\leq\sup_{\xi\in S}|(\hat{\phi},\xi)|+Q\log 2.

Recall from the proof of Lemma
8.12
that the law of
(\mathcal{C},\phi,\eta,+\infty,-\infty)/{\sim_{\gamma}}
is the restriction of
\mathcal{M}_{2}^{\mathrm{sph}}(W)\otimes\SLE_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{sep},\alpha}
to the event that the total quantum area is larger than
1
.
By Proposition
8.4
, conditioning on

(\mathcal{C}_{\eta}^{-},\phi)/{\sim_{\gamma}}
, the conditional law of
(\mathcal{C}_{\eta}^{+},\phi,+\infty)/{\sim_{\gamma}}
is
\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\mathrm{disk}}(\alpha;\mathfrak{l})^{\#}
, hence the conditional law of
(\mathcal{C}_{+},\hat{\phi},+\infty)/{\sim_{\gamma}}
is
\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\mathrm{disk}}(\alpha;1)^{\#}
.
Therefore, by (
8.14
) and (
8.15
), conditioning on
(\mathcal{C}_{\eta}^{-},\phi)/{\sim_{\gamma}}
,
the conditional probability of the event
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|(\phi^{0},g)-\frac{2}{\gamma}\log\mathfrak{l}}}\right|<-\frac{2\zeta}{\gamma}\log\mathfrak{l}
is at least
1-\mathrm{err}(\mathfrak{l})
. Since
g
is supported on
\{\Re z\in(C-1,C)\}
, if
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|(\phi^{0},g)-\frac{2}{\gamma}\log\mathfrak{l}}}\right|<-\frac{2\zeta}{\gamma}\log\mathfrak{l}
,
then there exists
s\in[C-1,C]
such that the average of
\phi^{0}
on
[s,s+2\pi i]
lies in
((1+\zeta)\frac{2}{\gamma}\log\mathfrak{l},(1-\zeta)\frac{2}{\gamma}\log\mathfrak{l})
. This gives
X_{\mathbf{t}+s}\in((1+\zeta)\frac{2}{\gamma}\log\mathfrak{l},(1-\zeta)\frac{2}{\gamma}\log\mathfrak{l})
hence
\mathbf{t}+s\in(\sigma_{{\mathfrak{l}}^{1-\zeta}},\tau_{{\mathfrak{l}}^{1+\zeta}})
for some
s\in[C-1,C]
. Therefore
\mathbf{t}\in(\sigma_{{\mathfrak{l}}^{1-\zeta}}-C,\tau_{{\mathfrak{l}}^{1+\zeta}})
, which gives Lemma
8.17
with our choice of
C
and
\mathrm{err}
.
∎

8.3.2
Proof of Proposition
8.14

We refer to Figure
9
and the paragraph above Section
8.3.1
for an illustration of our proof ideas.
We will write
o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1)
as an error term satisfying
\lim_{\delta\to 0}\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}|o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1)|=0
which can change from place to place.
We first prove the lower bound for
(M\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha})[E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}]
.

Lemma 8.18
.

We have
(M\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha})[E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}]\geq(1+o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1))|M||\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}|\frac{2\log\varepsilon^{-1}}{\gamma(Q-\alpha)}.

We will prove Lemma
8.18

by a coupling of the probability measure
M^{\#}=M/|M|
and cylindrical GFF measure
P_{\mathcal{C}}
.
Recall that for a sample
h
from
P_{\mathcal{C}}
, it can be written as

h=h_{1}+h_{2}
, where
h_{1}
is constant on vertical circles and
h_{2}
is the lateral component that has mean zero on all such
circles.

Lemma 8.19
.

There exists a coupling of
h
sampled from
P_{\mathcal{C}}
and
\phi
sampled from
M^{\#}

such that
h_{2}
is independent of
(X_{s})_{s\geq 0}
, and moreover,
\sup_{\Re z>1}|{\mathfrak{h}}(z)|<\infty
where
\mathfrak{h}(z)=\phi(z)-h_{2}(z)-X_{\Re z}
.

Proof.

By
[
MS17
, Proposition 2.8]
, on
\mathcal{C}_{+}
the field
h
can be written as
h_{0}+\tilde{\mathfrak{h}}
where
h_{0}
is a zero boundary GFF on
\mathcal{C}_{+}
and
\tilde{\mathfrak{h}}
is an independent harmonic function on
\mathcal{C}_{+}
such that
\sup_{\Re z>1}|\tilde{\mathfrak{h}}(z)|<\infty
.
Similarly, by Lemma
8.15
,
\phi(z)+(Q-\alpha)\Re z
on
\mathcal{C}_{+}
can be written as
\phi_{0}+\hat{\mathfrak{h}}
with the same properties.
Coupling
h
and
\phi
such that
h_{0}=\phi_{0}
and
\tilde{\mathfrak{h}}
is independent of
\phi
, we are done.
∎

Proof of Lemma
8.18
.

Let
\mathbbm{P}
be the probability measure corresponding to the law of
(\phi,h)
as coupled in Lemma
8.19
.
Let
(\phi,h,\mathbf{t},\eta^{0})
be a sample from
\mathbbm{P}\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}
.
Then the law of
(\phi,\mathbf{t},\eta^{0})
is

M^{\#}\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}
, where
M^{\#}=M/|M|
. Let
h_{2}
and
\mathfrak{h}
be defined
as in Lemma
8.19
so that
\phi=h_{2}+X_{\Re\cdot}+\mathfrak{h}
.
Fix
\zeta\in(0,0.1)
which will be sent to zero later.
Let
I_{\delta,\varepsilon}
be the interval
((1+3\zeta)\frac{2}{\gamma(Q-\alpha)}\log\delta^{-1}+\delta^{-1},(1-3\zeta)\frac{2}{\gamma(Q-\alpha)}\log\varepsilon^{-1})
.
Let

G_{\delta,\varepsilon}:=\{I_{\delta,\varepsilon}\subset(\tau_{\delta^{1+2\zeta}}+\delta^{-1},\sigma_{\varepsilon^{1-2\zeta}})\quad\textrm{and}\quad\sup_{\Re z>1}|{\mathfrak{h}}(z)|<\zeta\cdot\frac{2}{\gamma}\log\delta^{-1}\},

where the random times
\sigma_{y},\tau_{y}
are as in (
8.12
).
By Lemma
8.16
we have
\mathbbm{P}[I_{\delta,\varepsilon}\subset(\tau_{\delta^{1+2\zeta}}+\delta^{-1},\sigma_{\varepsilon^{1-2\zeta}})]=1-o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1)
.
Since
\sup_{\Re z>1}|{\mathfrak{h}}(z)|<\infty
almost surely, we have
\mathbbm{P}[G_{\delta,\varepsilon}]=1-o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1)
.

Fix
n>0
. Let
A_{n}=\{\inf_{z\in\eta^{0}}\Re z>-n\}
. Then
\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa}[A_{n}]<\infty
. Let
\eta=\eta^{0}+\mathbf{t}
and
\ell_{h_{2}}(\eta)

be the quantum length of
\eta
with respect to
h_{2}
. Define the event

E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(n):=\{A_{n}\textrm{ occurs},\quad\mathbf{t}\in I_{\delta,\varepsilon},\quad\textrm{and}\quad\ell_{h_{2}}(\eta)\in(\varepsilon^{\zeta},\delta^{-\zeta})\}.

Suppose
n<\delta^{-1}<\frac{2\zeta}{\gamma(Q-\alpha)}\log\varepsilon^{-1}
and the event
E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(n)\cap G_{\delta,\varepsilon}
occurs.
Since
\mathbf{t}\in I_{\delta,\varepsilon}
and
G_{\delta,\varepsilon}\cap A_{n}
occurs, we have
\Re z\in(\tau_{\delta^{1+2\zeta}},\sigma_{\varepsilon^{1-2\zeta}})
for each
z\in\eta
, hence
\mathcal{C}_{-}:=\{z\in\mathcal{C}:\Re z\leq 0\}\subset D^{-}_{\eta}
.
By the definition of
\phi
we have

\mu_{\phi}(\mathcal{C}_{\eta}^{-})\geq\mu_{\phi}(\mathcal{C}_{-})\geq 1

(8.16)

Moreover, from the bound on
\sup_{\Re z>1}|{\mathfrak{h}}(z)|
we have
\frac{\gamma}{2}(X_{\Re z}+\mathfrak{h}(z))\in((1-\zeta)\log\varepsilon,(1+\zeta)\log\delta)
for each
z\in\eta
.
Now
\ell_{h_{2}}(\eta)\in(\varepsilon^{\zeta},\delta^{-\zeta})
yields
\ell_{\phi}(\eta)\in(\varepsilon,\delta)
.
This gives

E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(n)\cap G_{\delta,\varepsilon}\subset E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}=\{\mu_{\phi}(\mathcal{C}_{\eta}^{-})\geq 1,\ell_{\phi}(\eta)\in(\varepsilon,\delta)\}

(8.17)

Therefore

(\mathbbm{P}\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha})[E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}]\geq(\mathbbm{P}\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha})[E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(n)]-(\mathbbm{P}\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha})[E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(n)\setminus G_{\delta,\varepsilon}].

(8.18)

We claim that

(\mathbbm{P}\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha})[E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(n)]=(1-o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1))|I_{\delta,\varepsilon}|\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa}[A_{n}]\textrm{ and }(\mathbbm{P}\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha})[E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(n)\setminus G_{\delta,\varepsilon}]=o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1)|I_{\delta,\varepsilon}|.

(8.19)

Since the law of
h_{2}
is translation invariant, namely
h_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}h_{2}(\cdot-t)
for each
t\in\mathbbm{R}
, we have

\displaystyle(\mathbbm{P}\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha})[E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(n)]=|I_{\delta,\varepsilon}|(\mathbbm{P}\times\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa})[\ell_{h_{2}}(\eta^{0})\in(\varepsilon^{\zeta},\delta^{-\zeta}),A_{n}]

\displaystyle=|I_{\delta,\varepsilon}|(\mathbbm{P}\times\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa})[A_{n}]-|I_{\delta,\varepsilon}|(\mathbbm{P}\times\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa})[\ell_{h_{2}}(\eta^{0})\notin(\varepsilon^{\zeta},\delta^{-\zeta}),A_{n}]=(1-o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1))|I_{\delta,\varepsilon}|\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa}[A_{n}].

In the last line we used
(\mathbbm{P}\times\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa})[A_{n}]=\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa}[A_{n}]<\infty
and
(\mathbbm{P}\times\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa})[\ell_{h_{2}}(\eta^{0})\notin(\varepsilon^{\zeta},\delta^{-\zeta})|A_{n}]=o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1)
, the latter of which holds because
\ell_{h_{2}}(\eta^{0})<\infty
a.e. in
\mathbbm{P}\times\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa}
.
This gives the first identity in (
8.19
).

The second identity in (
8.19
) is proved by as follows:

\displaystyle(\mathbbm{P}\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha})[E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(n)\setminus G_{\delta,\varepsilon}]\leq(\mathbbm{P}\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha})[A_{n}\setminus G_{\delta,\varepsilon}\textrm{ and }\mathbf{t}\in I_{\delta,\varepsilon}]

\displaystyle=\mathbbm{P}[G_{\delta,\varepsilon}\textrm{ does not occur}]\times|I_{\delta,\varepsilon}|\times\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa}[A_{n}]=o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1)|I_{\delta,\varepsilon}|\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa}[A_{n}].

By (
8.18
) and (
8.19
), for a fixed
n
,
we have
(\mathbbm{P}\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha})[E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}]\geq(1-o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1))|I_{\delta,\varepsilon}||\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa}[A_{n}]
hence

\frac{1}{|M|}(M\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha})[E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}]\geq(1-o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1))|I_{\delta,\varepsilon}|\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa}[A_{n}]\geq(1-o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1))\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa}[A_{n}](1-4\zeta)\frac{2\log\varepsilon^{-1}}{\gamma(Q-\alpha)}.

Sending
\varepsilon\to 0
,
\delta\to 0
, and
\zeta\to 0
in order, we see that

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\liminf_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{(M\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha})[E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}]\times\gamma(Q-\alpha)}{2\log\varepsilon^{-1}}\geq|M|\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa}[A_{n}].

Since
\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa}[A_{n}]\rightarrow|\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa}|
, sending
n\to\infty
we conclude the proof.
∎

It remains to prove the upper bound for
(M\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha})[E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}]
.

Lemma 8.20
.

We have
(M\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha})[E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}]\leq(1+o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1))|M||\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}|\frac{2\log\varepsilon^{-1}}{\gamma(Q-\alpha)}.

Proof.

Recall
\sigma
and
\tau
defined in (
8.12
). For
C>0
, consider the event

F_{x,y,C}=\{(\phi,\mathbf{t},\eta^{0})\>:\>\mathbf{t}\in(\sigma_{x}-C,\tau_{y})\}.

(8.20)

We will prove Lemma
8.20
by first bounding
(M\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha})[F_{x,y,C}]
and then comparing
E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}
to
F_{\delta^{1-\zeta},\varepsilon^{1+\zeta},C}
.
More precisely, we will prove two estimates. First, for each
x>0
, we have

(M\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha})[F_{x,y,C}]\leq(1+o_{y}(1))|M||\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}|(\frac{2\log y^{-1}}{\gamma(Q-\alpha)}+C)\quad\textrm{as }y\to 0,

(8.21)

where
o_{y}(1)\to 0
uniformly in
C
.
Moreover, for a fixed
\zeta\in(0,1)
there exists some
C>0
such that

(M\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha})[F_{\delta^{1-\zeta},\varepsilon^{1+\zeta},C}\mid E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}]=1-o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1).

(8.22)

Given (
8.21
) and (
8.22
), we see that for any fixed
\zeta>0
there exists some
C>0
such that

(M\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha})[E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}]\leq(1+o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1))(M\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha})[F_{\delta^{1-\zeta},\varepsilon^{1+\zeta},C}]\leq(1+o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1))|M||\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}|\frac{(1+\zeta)2\log\varepsilon^{-1}}{\gamma(Q-\alpha)},

Sending
\zeta\to 0
we will get Lemma
8.20
.

We first prove (
8.21
). Note that

(M\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}^{\alpha})[F_{x,y,C}]=M^{\#}[\tau_{y}-\sigma_{x}+C]|M||\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa}|
from the definition of
F_{x,y,C}
.
Hence (
8.21
) is equivalent to
M^{\#}[\tau_{y}-\sigma_{x}]\leq(1+o_{y}(1))\frac{2\log y^{-1}}{\gamma(Q-\alpha)}
.
By Lemma
8.15
, the process
X_{s}
evolves as Brownian motion with drift
-(Q-\alpha)
.
Let
T=\inf\{s\geq 0:B^{x}_{s}-(Q-\alpha)s=\frac{2}{\gamma}\log y\}
where
(B^{x}_{s})_{s\geq 0}
is a Brownian motion starting from
\frac{2}{\gamma}\log x
.
Then
M^{\#}[\tau_{y}-\sigma_{x}]\leq\mathbbm{E}[T]
. Since
\mathbbm{E}[T]=(1+o_{y}(1))\frac{2\log y^{-1}}{\gamma(Q-\alpha)}
, we get (
8.21
).

Since the event
E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}
is determined by
(\mathcal{C}_{\eta}^{-},\phi)/{\sim_{\gamma}}
, and
\ell_{\phi}(\eta)\in(\varepsilon,\delta)
on
E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}
, Lemma
8.17
yields (
8.22
)
with the constant
C
from Lemma
8.17
. This concludes the proof.
∎

8.4
Area statistics of generalized quantum disks

We now transition from the
\kappa<4
regime to the
\kappa^{\prime}\in(4,8)
setting, with the goal of proving Proposition
8.2
in Section
8.5
.
We review several results on the law of forested disks, mainly from
[
HL22
]
. In order to use the results there, we first need to clarify the equivalence between our
\mathcal{M}^{\rm f.d.}
as in Definition
2.32
and the definitions of generalized quantum disks in
[
HL22
]
.

We first recall the notion of looptree, which is originally defined in
[
DMS21
]
.
Suppose
\nu\in(1,2)
. Let
(\zeta_{t})_{t\geq 0}
be a stable Lévy process with exponent
\nu
with no negative jumps started at
\zeta_{0}=0
. This only specifies the process up to a multiplicative constant, which we fix by requiring
\mathbbm{E}[e^{-\lambda\zeta_{t}}]=e^{-t\lambda^{\nu}}
for
\lambda,t>0
. Equivalently,
(\zeta_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is the
\nu
-stable Lévy process whose jump measure is
\frac{1}{\Gamma(-\nu)}h^{-1-\nu}1_{h>0}dh
. One can make sense of the
Lévy excursion conditioned to have duration
\ell
by conditioning on the event that
\zeta_{t}
first hits
-\varepsilon
at time
\ell
, then sending
\varepsilon\to 0
. See e.g.
[
DLG02
, Section 1]
for further discussion.

Definition 8.21

(
[
MSW21
]
)
.

Let
e:[0,\ell]\to\mathbbm{R}
be a sample from the
\nu
-stable Lévy excursion with no negative jumps conditioned on having duration
\ell
.
On the graph
\{(t,e(t)):t\in[0,\ell]\}
we define an equivalence relation
s\sim t
iff
e(s)=e(t)
and the horizontal segment connecting
(s,e(s))
and
(t,e(t))
is below the graph of
e|_{s,t}
. We also set
(t,e(t))
and
(t,e(t-))
to be equivalent if
t
is a jump time of
e
; this naturally identifies a root for each loop. We call the quotient
\mathcal{T}_{\ell}
of the graph
\{(t,e(t)):t\in[0,\ell]\}
under the above equivalence relation the
\nu
-stable looptree
with length
\ell
.

Note that the looptree
\mathcal{T}_{\ell}
defined above is naturally rooted at the equivalence class of the origin
(0,0)
. We define the
generalized boundary length measure
on the looptree to be the pushforward of the Lebesgue measure on
[0,\ell]
.

Figure 10:
From a Lévy excursion to a looptree. The root of
\mathcal{T}_{\ell}
is marked as the dot.

Now we review the definitions of generalized quantum disks in
[
HL22
]
. According to
[
HL22
, (1.8)]
, the authors define the generalized quantum disk with one
\alpha
-bulk marked point and one boundary marked point as

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1,0,1}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;\ell)=\sum_{t:\Delta e_{t}\neq 0}\frac{1}{|\mathrm{QD}_{0,1}(|\Delta e_{t}|)|}\mathcal{M}_{1,1}^{\rm disk}(\alpha;|\Delta e_{t}|)\prod_{s\neq t:\Delta e_{s}\neq 0}\mathrm{QD}_{0,1}^{\#}(|\Delta e_{s}|)\,d\mathcal{T}_{\ell},

(8.23)

where
d\mathcal{T}_{\ell}
denotes for the probability measure of
\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}
-stable loop tree with length
\ell
. Here, the summation is over the set of jumps of the Lévy excursion
e
defining
\mathcal{T}_{\ell}
. A sample from the RHS is a looptree
\mathcal{T}_{\ell}
, a quantum disk with a
\alpha
-bulk insertion and a marked boundary point, and a collection of quantum disks each with a marked boundary point. Identifying the boundaries of the quantum surfaces with the corresponding loops according to boundary length gives a generalized quantum surface with a marked bulk point and a marked boundary point (given by the root of
\mathcal{T}_{\ell}
); we denote its law by
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1,0,1}^{\mathrm{f.d.}}(\alpha;\ell)
.
We denote the law of a sample from
\ell^{-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1,0,1}^{\mathrm{f.d.}}(\alpha;\ell)
with boundary point forgotten by
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1,0,0}^{\mathrm{f.d.}}(\alpha;\ell)
.

Lemma 8.22
.

We have
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1,0,1}^{\rm f.d.}(\gamma;\ell)\ell^{-1-\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}}=R_{\gamma}^{\prime-1}\frac{2\pi(Q-\gamma)^{2}}{\gamma}\mathrm{GQD}_{1,1}(\ell)
.

Proof.

As explained above Proposition
2.29
, our
\mathrm{GQD}_{0,1}(\ell)^{\#}
is same as in
[
HL22
]
, i.e. the probability measure
\prod_{s:\Delta e_{s}\neq 0}\mathrm{QD}_{0,1}^{\#}(|\Delta e_{s}|)\,d\mathcal{T}_{\ell}
. Take the total mass into consideration, by Lemma
2.28
we deduce
\mathrm{GQD}_{0,1}(\ell)=R_{\gamma}^{\prime}\ell^{-1-\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}}\cdot\prod_{s:\Delta e_{s}\neq 0}\mathrm{QD}_{0,1}^{\#}(|\Delta e_{s}|)\,d\mathcal{T}_{\ell}
. Then on both sides we weigh the total quantum area and sample one point according to the probability measure proportional to the quantum area measure; the LHS is just
\mathrm{GQD}_{1,1}(\ell)
and the RHS gives

R_{\gamma}^{\prime}\ell^{-1-\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}}\sum_{t:\Delta e_{t}\neq 0}\frac{1}{|\mathrm{QD}_{0,1}(|\Delta e_{t}|)|}\mathrm{QD}_{1,0}(|\Delta e_{t}|)\prod_{s\neq t:\Delta e_{s}\neq 0}\mathrm{QD}_{0,1}^{\#}(|\Delta e_{s}|)\,d\mathcal{T}_{\ell}.

(8.24)

Since by Theorem
2.22
one has
\mathrm{QD}_{1,0}(\ell)=\frac{\gamma}{2\pi(Q-\gamma)^{2}}\mathcal{M}_{1,0}^{\mathrm{disk}}(\gamma;\ell)
, according to (
8.23
), (
8.24
) above is just equal to
R_{\gamma}^{\prime}\ell^{-1-\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}}\frac{\gamma}{2\pi(Q-\gamma)^{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1,0,1}^{\rm f.d.}(\gamma;\ell)
.
∎

According to the above lemma, we can conclude

Proposition 8.23
.

The definitions of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1,0,1}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;\ell)
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;\ell)
above are consistent with
\mathcal{M}_{1,0,1}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;\ell)
and
\mathcal{M}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;\ell)
in Definition
2.32
, namely, we have

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1,0,1}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;\ell)\ell^{-1-\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}}=C\mathcal{M}_{1,0,1}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;\ell),\quad\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;\ell)\ell^{-2-\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}}=C\mathcal{M}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;\ell),

(8.25)

where
C
only depends on
\gamma
.

Proof.

The proof is by using a reweighting argument on Lemma
8.22
. That is, according to Lemma
8.22
and Proposition
2.34
, we have

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1,0,1}^{\mathrm{f.d.}}(\gamma;\ell)\ell^{-1-\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}}=C\mathcal{M}_{1,0,1}^{\mathrm{f.d.}}(\gamma;\ell)

(8.26)

with a
\gamma
-dependent constant
C
. On both sides of (
8.26
), if we reweight the bulk marked point of weight
\gamma
to be of weight
\alpha
, then the LHS turns out to be
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1,0,1}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;\ell)\ell^{-1-\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}}
, while the RHS to be exactly
C\mathcal{M}_{1,0,1}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;\ell)
. Then the first claim follows; by reweighting
\ell^{-1}
and forgetting the boundary marked point the second claim follows straightforwardly.
∎

According to the equivalence in Proposition
8.23
, we can compute the total mass of
\mathcal{M}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;\ell)
based on
[
HL22
]
.

Proposition 8.24
.

For
\alpha>\frac{\gamma}{2}
, we have
|\mathcal{M}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;\ell)|=D(\alpha)|\mathcal{M}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\gamma;1)|\ell^{\frac{\gamma}{2}(\alpha-Q)-1}
, where

D(\alpha):=\frac{|\mathcal{M}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;1)|}{|\mathcal{M}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\gamma;1)|}=(2\pi)^{2-2\alpha/\gamma}2^{2-\alpha Q+\alpha^{2}/2}\frac{\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2\alpha}{\gamma}-\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}}}}\right)}{\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2-\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}}}}\right)}\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}}}\right)^{2\alpha/\gamma-2}.

Proof.

According to Proposition
8.23
, our definition of
\mathcal{M}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;1)
only differs from
[
HL22
]
by a
\gamma
-dependent constant, i.e. we have
\frac{|\mathcal{M}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;1)|}{|\mathcal{M}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\gamma;1)|}=\frac{|\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;1)|}{|\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\gamma;1)|}
. In the following
we will prove

|\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;1)|=\frac{2}{\gamma}2^{-\alpha^{2}/2}\bar{U}(\alpha)\frac{\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}}}\right)\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2\alpha}{\gamma}-\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}}}}\right)}{\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}}}}\right)\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\gamma\alpha}{2}-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}}}\right)}\frac{1}{\bar{R}(\gamma;1,1)}.

(8.27)

Given this and the formulas for
\bar{U}(\alpha)
in Proposition
2.18
and
\bar{R}(\gamma;1,1)
in Proposition
2.16
, the result follows.
According to (
8.23
), the total mass of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;1)
equals

|\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;1)|=\mathbbm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sum_{(t,\Delta e_{t})}\frac{1}{|\mathrm{QD}_{0,1}(\Delta e_{t})|}\big|\mathcal{M}_{1,1}^{\rm disk}(\alpha;\Delta e_{t})\big|}}\right]

where
\mathbbm{E}
denotes expectation with respect to the probability measure of the
\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}
-stable Lévy excursion with living time
1
.
According to the expressions of
|\mathrm{QD}_{0,1}(\Delta e_{t})|
and
|\mathcal{M}_{1,1}^{\rm disk}(\alpha;\Delta e_{t})\big|
in Propositions
2.16
and
2.18
, we deduce

|\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;1)|=\frac{2}{\gamma}2^{-\alpha^{2}/2}\bar{U}(\alpha)\mathbbm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sum_{t}|\Delta e_{t}|^{2\alpha/\gamma}}}\right]\frac{1}{\bar{R}(\gamma;1,1)}

(8.28)

The expectation
\mathbbm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sum_{t}|\Delta e_{t}|^{2\alpha/\gamma}}}\right]
can be computed by applying
[
HL22
, Lemma 4.2]
. Namely, choose
G(x)=0
in Proposition
[
HL22
, Lemma 4.2]
, then for
\lambda>0
and
F:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)
twice continuously differentable with
F(0)=F^{\prime}(0)=0
,
[
HL22
, Formula (4.3)]
gives

-\frac{d}{d\lambda}(e^{-\rho(\lambda)})\frac{1}{\Gamma(-\nu)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{dh}{h^{1+\nu}}e^{-\rho(\lambda)h}F(h)=\frac{e^{-\rho(\lambda)}}{\nu\Gamma(1-1/\nu)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\ell}{\ell^{1+1/\nu}}\mathbbm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{t\leq 1}F(\ell^{1/\nu}\Delta e_{t})e^{-\lambda\ell}}}\right)\;

(8.29)

where
\rho(\lambda)=\lambda^{\mathbf{1}/\nu}
. Setting
F(x)=x^{2\alpha/\gamma}
in (
8.29
) and simplifying gives

e^{-\rho(\lambda)}\frac{1}{\nu}\lambda^{\mathbf{1}/\nu-1}\frac{1}{\Gamma(-\nu)}\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\nu+\frac{2\alpha}{\gamma}}}\right)\rho(\lambda)^{\nu-\frac{2\alpha}{\gamma}}=\frac{e^{-\rho(\lambda)}}{\nu\Gamma(1-1/\nu)}\lambda^{-\frac{2\alpha}{\gamma\nu}+\frac{1}{\nu}}\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2\alpha}{\gamma\nu}-\frac{1}{\nu}}}\right)\mathbbm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sum_{t}|\Delta e_{t}|^{2\alpha/\gamma}}}\right],

i.e. we have

\mathbbm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sum_{t}|\Delta e_{t}|^{2\alpha/\gamma}}}\right]=\frac{\Gamma(1-1/\nu)\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\nu+\frac{2\alpha}{\gamma}}}\right)}{\Gamma(-\nu)\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2\alpha}{\gamma\nu}-\frac{1}{\nu}}}\right)}=\frac{\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}}}\right)\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2\alpha}{\gamma}-\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}}}}\right)}{\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}}}}\right)\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\gamma\alpha}{2}-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}}}\right)}.

(8.30)

Plugging (
8.30
) into (
8.28
) gives (
8.27
), which gives the result.
∎

Finally, we record the Laplace transform of the quantum area of
\mathcal{M}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;\ell)^{\#}
in
[
HL22
]
.

Proposition 8.25

(
[
HL22
, Theorem 1.8]
)
.

Let
M^{\prime}:=2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\mu}{4\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4}}}}\right)^{\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{8}}
. For
\alpha\in(\frac{\gamma}{2},Q)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;\ell)^{\#}[e^{-\mu A}]=\frac{2}{\Gamma(\frac{\gamma}{2}(Q-\alpha))}(\frac{M^{\prime}\ell}{2})^{\frac{\gamma}{2}(Q-\alpha)}K_{\frac{\gamma}{2}(Q-\alpha)}(M^{\prime}\ell)

(8.31)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;\ell)^{\#}[Ae^{-\mu A}]=\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{2\mu\Gamma(\frac{\gamma}{2}(Q-\alpha))}(\frac{M^{\prime}\ell}{2})^{\frac{\gamma}{2}(Q-\alpha)+1}K_{1-\frac{\gamma}{2}(Q-\alpha)}(M^{\prime}\ell)

(8.32)

In particular,

\mathrm{GQD}_{1,0}(\ell)^{\#}[e^{-\mu A}]=\bar{K}_{1-4/\kappa^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2l\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\mu}{4\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4}}}}\right)^{\kappa^{\prime}/8}}}\right).

8.5
The
\kappa^{\prime}\in(4,8)
case

In this section, we prove Proposition
8.2
. The proof of Proposition
8.1
in Section
8.1
used Proposition
8.4
, Lemma
8.5
and Proposition
8.6
. The analogous inputs in our setting are Proposition
8.26
, Lemma
8.28
and Proposition
8.29
below, and given these, the proof of Proposition
8.2
is identical to that of Proposition
8.1
.
As usual, we assume
\kappa^{\prime}\in(4,8)
and
\gamma=16/\sqrt{\kappa^{\prime}}
.

Sample
\eta
from
\mathcal{L}_{\kappa^{\prime}}
, we define the connected component that contains
+\infty
as
\mathcal{D}_{\eta^{+}}
, with
\eta^{+}
as its boundary and we also define the connected component contains
-\infty
as
\mathcal{D}_{\eta^{-}}
, with
\eta^{-}
as its boundary. Let
-\eta
be the image of
\eta
under reflection
z\mapsto-z
. Under these conventions, we have

\vartheta(\eta)=-\log\mathrm{CR}(\exp(\eta^{+}),0)-\log\mathrm{CR}(\exp(-\eta^{-}),0)

d\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})(\eta)=2^{2\lambda}e^{\lambda\vartheta(\eta)}{d\mathcal{L}_{\kappa^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})}(\eta)

Then the goal of this section is to compute
|\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})|
for
\kappa^{\prime}\in(4,8)
.

Recall for a sample
(\eta,{\bf t})
from
\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa^{\prime}}\times dt
.
\SLE^{\rm sep,\alpha}_{\kappa^{\prime}}
is the law of the translated loop
\eta+{\bf t}
. We have the following conformal welding result, we prove it in Section
8.6
later.

Proposition 8.26
.

There is a constant
C=C(\gamma)
such that for all
\alpha\in(\frac{\gamma}{2},Q)
we have

C(Q-\alpha)^{2}\mathcal{M}_{2}^{\rm sph}(\alpha)\otimes\SLE^{\rm sep,\alpha}_{\kappa^{\prime}}=\int_{0}^{\infty}\ell\cdot\mathrm{Weld}(\mathcal{M}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;\ell),\mathcal{M}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;\ell))d\ell.

(8.33)

For
0<\varepsilon<\delta
, let
E_{\delta,\varepsilon}
be the event that the generalized quantum surface containing the first marked point has quantum area at least
1
and
the loop has generalized quantum length in
(\varepsilon,\delta)
. As in the simple case, we will evaluate event
E_{\delta,\varepsilon}
on both sides of (
8.33
) to deduce a formula for
|\mathcal{L}_{\kappa^{\prime}}^{\alpha}|
. To evaluate
E_{\delta,\varepsilon}
on the right hand side of
8.33
, we need the following lemma.

Lemma 8.27
.

Writing
A
to denote quantum area, we have the following tail asymptotics

\displaystyle\mathrm{GQD}_{1,0}(1)^{\#}[A>x]=\frac{\Gamma(4/\kappa^{\prime})}{\Gamma(2-4/\kappa^{\prime})\Gamma(2-\kappa^{\prime}/4)}(4\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4})^{-\kappa^{\prime}/4+1}x^{-\kappa^{\prime}/4+1}(1+o(1))

(8.34)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;1)^{\#}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[A>x}}\right]=\frac{\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\alpha\gamma}{2}-\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}}}\right)}{\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2-\frac{\alpha\gamma}{2}+\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}}}\right)\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2\alpha}{\gamma}-\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(4\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4}}}\right)^{-1-\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}+\frac{2\alpha}{\gamma}}x^{-1-\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}+\frac{2\alpha}{\gamma}}(1+o(1)).

(8.35)

where
o(1)
satisfies
\lim_{x\to 0}o(1)=0
.

Proof.

We only prove the first equation, since the argument for the second one is identical. According to Proposition
2.29
, we have
\mathrm{GQD}_{1,0}(1)^{\#}[e^{-\mu A}]=\bar{K}_{1-4/\kappa^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M^{\prime}}}\right)
for
M^{\prime}=2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\mu}{4\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4}}}}\right)^{\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{8}}
. Now we need to estimate its inverse Laplace transform. According to Post’s inversion formula
[
Pos30
]
, for a bounded continuous function
f:[0,\infty)\to\mathbbm{R}
, its Laplace transform
F(s)=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-st}f(t)dt
exists and is smooth for
s>0
, and we can uniquely recover
f
as

\mathcal{L}^{-1}\{F\}(t)=\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{k}{t}}}\right)^{k+1}F^{(k)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{k}{t}}}\right).

(8.36)

Now we use (
8.36
) to obtain the leading term of inverse Laplace transform of
\mathrm{GQD}_{1,0}(1)^{\#}[e^{-\mu A}]=\bar{K}_{1-4/\kappa^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M^{\prime}}}\right)
. Note that we have the absolutely convergent expansion (see e.g.
[
AS48
, (9.6.2), (9.6.10)]

\bar{K}_{\nu}(z)=\Gamma(1-\nu)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!\Gamma(n-\nu+1)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{z}{2}}}\right)^{2n}-\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!\Gamma(n+\nu+1)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{z}{2}}}\right)^{2n+2\nu}}}\right],

hence we have

\mathrm{GQD}(1)^{\#}[e^{-\mu A}]=\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n+\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\mu}{4\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4}}}}\right)^{n\cdot\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}}-\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(n-1)!\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n-\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}+1}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\mu}{4\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4}}}}\right)^{n\cdot\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}-1}}}\right],

(8.37)

and we can evaluate its
k
-th derivative as (one can easily check the condition of term by term differentiation holds)

\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}+1}}\right)}{n!\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n+\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}}}\right)\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}-k+1}}\right)}\frac{\mu^{n\cdot\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}-k}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(4\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4}}}\right)^{n\cdot\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}}}-\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}}}\right)}{(n-1)!\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n-\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}+1}}\right)\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}-k}}\right)}\frac{\mu^{n\cdot\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}-1-k}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(4\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4}}}\right)^{n\cdot\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}-1}}}}\right].

(8.38)

We now take (
8.38
) into the formula (
8.36
). Using the fact
\Gamma(z-n)\sim(-)^{n}\frac{\pi}{\sin\pi z}\frac{n^{z-1}}{\Gamma(n)}
(see e.g.
[
AS48
, (6.1.46), (6.1.17)]
), we find the limit in (
8.36
) is given by

-\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}+1}}\right)}{n!\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n+\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}}}\right)}\frac{\sin\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\pi\frac{n\kappa^{\prime}}{4}}}\right)}{\pi}\frac{t^{-n\cdot\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}-1}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(4\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4}}}\right)^{n\cdot\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}}}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}}}\right)}{(n-1)!\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n-\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}+1}}\right)}\frac{\sin\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\pi\frac{n\kappa^{\prime}}{4}}}\right)}{\pi}\frac{t^{-n\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(4\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4}}}\right)^{n\cdot\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}-1}}}}\right].

(8.39)

Note that the
n=1
term in the second summation above gives the leading order of (
8.39
) as
t\to\infty
. Therefore the inversion of
\mathrm{GQD}_{0,1}(1)^{\#}[e^{-\mu A}]
has the asymptotic:

-\frac{\Gamma(4/\kappa^{\prime})}{\pi\Gamma(2-4/\kappa^{\prime})}(4\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4})^{-\kappa^{\prime}/4+1}\sin\frac{\pi\kappa^{\prime}}{4}\Gamma(\kappa^{\prime}/4)t^{-\kappa^{\prime}/4}+O(t^{-1-\kappa^{\prime}/4}).

(8.40)

Finally, the tail probability follows just by the integration of (
8.40
) on
[x,\infty)
, which leads to

\displaystyle\mathrm{GQD}_{1,0}(1)^{\#}[A>x]

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma(4/\kappa^{\prime})}{\pi\Gamma(2-4/\kappa^{\prime})}(4\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4})^{-\kappa^{\prime}/4+1}\Gamma(\kappa^{\prime}/4)\frac{1}{-\kappa^{\prime}/4+1}\sin\frac{\pi\kappa^{\prime}}{4}x^{-\kappa^{\prime}/4+1}+O(x^{-\kappa^{\prime}/4})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma(4/\kappa^{\prime})}{\Gamma(2-4/\kappa^{\prime})\Gamma(2-\kappa^{\prime}/4)}(4\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4})^{-\kappa^{\prime}/4+1}x^{-\kappa^{\prime}/4+1}+O(x^{-\kappa^{\prime}/4}),

which gives the result.
∎

We now evaluate the measure of the event
E_{\delta,\varepsilon}
with respect to the right hand side of (
8.33
):

Lemma 8.28
.

With
D(\alpha)
the constant in Proposition
8.24
, we have

\displaystyle(\mathcal{M}_{2}^{\rm sph}(\alpha)\otimes\SLE_{\kappa^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{sep},\alpha})[E_{\delta,\varepsilon}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C^{\prime}\times\frac{D(\alpha)^{2}}{(Q-\alpha)^{2}}\frac{\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\alpha\gamma}{2}-\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}}}\right)}{\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2-\frac{\alpha\gamma}{2}+\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}}}\right)\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2\alpha}{\gamma}-\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(4\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4}}}\right)^{-1-\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}+\frac{2\alpha}{\gamma}}(1+o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1))\log\varepsilon^{-1}

where
C^{\prime}
is a constant only depending on
\gamma
.

Proof.

Similar to the computation in Lemma
8.5
, we have

\displaystyle(\mathcal{M}_{2}^{\mathrm{sph}}(W)\otimes\SLE_{\kappa^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{sep},\alpha})[E_{\delta,\varepsilon}]=\frac{1}{C(Q-\alpha)^{2}}\int_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\ell|\mathcal{M}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;\ell)|^{2}\mathcal{M}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;1)^{\#}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[A>\ell^{-8/\kappa^{\prime}}}}\right]d\ell

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C^{\prime}\times\frac{D(\alpha)^{2}}{(Q-\alpha)^{2}}\frac{\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\alpha\gamma}{2}-\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}}}\right)}{\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2-\frac{\alpha\gamma}{2}+\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}}}\right)\Gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2\alpha}{\gamma}-\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(4\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4}}}\right)^{-1-\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{4}+\frac{2\alpha}{\gamma}}(1+o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1))\log\varepsilon^{-1}

where we use Lemma
8.27
to obtain the last expression.
∎

Next, we evaluate the measure of
E_{\delta,\varepsilon}
with respect to the left hand side of (
8.33
):

Proposition 8.29
.

Let
\alpha\in(\frac{\gamma}{2},Q)
. We have

(\mathcal{M}_{2}^{\mathrm{sph}}(W)\otimes\SLE_{\kappa^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{sep},\alpha})[E_{\delta,\varepsilon}]=(1+o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1))\frac{\gamma^{2}\overline{R}(\alpha)}{8(Q-\alpha)^{2}}|\mathcal{L}_{\kappa^{\prime}}^{\alpha}|\log\varepsilon^{-1}

where the reflection coefficient
\overline{R}(\alpha)
is as in Lemma
2.8
,

Proof.

As we explain, the argument is a minor modification of that of Section
8.3
.
Keeping the notation in that section, we sample
(\phi,{\bf t},\eta^{0})
from
M\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa^{\prime}}^{\alpha}
and set
\eta=\eta^{0}+{\bf t}
, let
\mathfrak{C}_{\eta}^{-}
be the region surrounded by
\eta
that contains
-\infty
, as defined in the paragraph above Lemma
6.17
. Define the event

E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}=\{(\phi,\eta):\varepsilon<\ell_{\phi}(\eta)<\delta\text{ and }\mu_{\phi}(\mathfrak{C}_{\eta}^{-})>1\}

where
\ell_{\phi}(\eta)
is the generalized quantum length of
\eta
.
Then it suffices to prove

\displaystyle(M\times dt\times\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\kappa^{\prime}})[E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}]=(1+o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1))|M||\mathcal{L}_{\kappa^{\prime}}^{\alpha}|\frac{\gamma\log\varepsilon^{-1}}{2(Q-\alpha)}.

(8.41)

Equation (
8.41
) is the
\kappa^{\prime}\in(4,8)
analog of Proposition
8.14
; indeed, the only difference in the right hand sides is that the factor
\frac{2}{\gamma}
in Proposition
8.14
becomes
\frac{\gamma}{2}
in (
8.41
). The reason for the change
\frac{2}{\gamma}\to\frac{\gamma}{2}
is that under
E^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon}
, the generalized quantum length
\ell_{\phi}(\eta^{0}+{\bf t})\approx e^{\frac{2}{\gamma}X_{\bf t}}
.

The proof of the upper bound in (
8.41
) is identical to the proof of Lemma
8.20
(upper bound of Proposition
8.14
), except that each factor
\frac{\gamma}{2}
or
\frac{2}{\gamma}
is replaced by its reciprocal, and the input Lemma
8.17
is replaced with the following:

Lemma 8.30
.

Sample
(\phi,\mathbf{t},\eta^{0})
from
M\times dt\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa^{\prime}}^{\alpha}
and set
\eta=\eta^{0}+\mathbf{t}
. Let
\mathfrak{l}=\ell_{\phi}(\eta)
, which is the boundary length of the beaded quantum surface
(\mathfrak{C}_{\eta}^{-},\phi)/{\sim_{\gamma}}
.
Fix
\zeta\in(0,1)
.
Then there exists a constant
C>0
and a function
\mathrm{err}(\ell)
such that
\lim_{\ell\downarrow 0}\mathrm{err}(\ell)=0
, and conditioned on

(\mathfrak{C}_{\eta}^{-},\phi)/{\sim_{\gamma}}
,
the conditional probability of
\{\mathbf{t}\in(\sigma_{{\mathfrak{l}}^{1-\zeta}}-C,\tau_{{\mathfrak{l}}^{1+\zeta}})\}
is at least
1-\mathrm{err}(\mathfrak{l})
. Where

\sigma_{y}=\inf\{s>0\>:\>X_{s}<\frac{\gamma}{2}\log y\}\quad\textrm{and}\quad\tau_{y}=\sup\{s>0\>:\>X_{s}>\frac{\gamma}{2}\log y\}.

(8.42)

and
\mathrm{err}(\mathfrak{l}):=\mathcal{M}^{\rm f.d.}_{1,0,0}(\alpha;1)^{\#}\Big[\sup_{\xi\in S}|(h,\xi)|+Q\log 2\geq-\frac{\gamma\zeta}{2}\log\ell\Big]
.

Proof.

The argument is identical to Lemma
8.17
, except we use Lemma
6.19
in place of Lemma
6.11
.
∎

The proof of the lower bound of (
8.41
) is identical to the proof of Lemma
8.18
(lower bound of Proposition
8.14
); the only changes needed are that each factor of
\frac{\gamma}{2}
or
\frac{2}{\gamma}
should be replaced by its reciprocal, and in (
8.16
) and (
8.17
)
\mathcal{C}_{\eta}^{-}
should be replaced by
\mathfrak{C}_{\eta}^{-}
. This concludes the proof of (
8.41
), so Proposition
8.29
holds.

∎

Proof of Proposition
8.2
.

The result quickly follows by evaluating
E_{\delta,\varepsilon}
on both sides of (
8.33
), and plugging in the expressions in Lemma
8.28
and Proposition
8.29
.
∎

8.6
Proof of Proposition
8.26

We start with the
\gamma
insertion case. The following lemma is the counterpart of Lemma
8.7
.

Lemma 8.31
.

For
(\phi,\eta)
sampled from
\mathrm{LF}_{\mathcal{C}}^{(\gamma,\pm\infty)}\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C})
, the law of
(\mathcal{C},\phi,\eta,-\infty,+\infty)/{\sim_{\gamma}}
is
C\int_{0}^{\infty}\ell\cdot\mathrm{Weld}(\mathcal{M}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\gamma;\ell),\mathcal{M}^{\rm f.d.}_{1,0,0}(\gamma;\ell))d\ell
for some
\gamma
-dependent constant
C\in(0,\infty)
.

Proof.

As in the proof of Lemma
8.7
, the result is immediate from Propositions
6.15
and
8.3
.
∎

Fix
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
and let
\eta
be a non-self-crossing loop separating
\pm\infty
. As at the beginning of Section
8.5
, we can define
\eta^{+}
and
\eta^{-}
. We mark a point
p^{+}
on
\eta^{+}
and
p^{-}
on
\eta^{-}
and let
\psi_{\eta^{\pm}}:\mathbb{H}\to\mathcal{C}_{\eta^{\pm}}
,
\psi_{\eta^{\pm}}(i)=\pm\infty
and
\psi_{\eta^{\pm}}(0)=p^{\pm}
. For
\phi
sampled from
\mathrm{LF}_{\mathcal{C}}^{(\gamma,\pm\infty)}
, let
X^{\pm}=\phi\circ\psi_{\eta^{\pm}}+Q\log|(\psi_{\eta^{\pm}})^{\prime}|
and
\mathcal{C}_{\eta,p^{\pm},\varepsilon}=\mathcal{C}\setminus(\psi_{\eta^{-}}(B_{\varepsilon}(i))\cup\psi_{\eta^{+}}(B_{\varepsilon}(i)))
.

Lemma 8.32
.

For a fixed
\alpha\in(\frac{\gamma}{2},Q)
and
for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
and any nonnegative measurable function
f
of
\phi|_{\mathcal{C}_{\eta,p^{\pm},\varepsilon}}
we have

\displaystyle\int f(\phi|_{\mathcal{C}_{\eta,p^{\pm},\varepsilon}})\times\varepsilon^{\alpha^{2}-\gamma^{2}}e^{(\alpha-\gamma)(X^{-}_{\varepsilon}(i)+X^{+}_{\varepsilon}(i))}\,d\mathrm{LF}_{\mathcal{C}}^{(\gamma,\pm\infty)}

\displaystyle=\int f(\phi|_{\mathcal{C}_{\eta,p^{\pm},\varepsilon}})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{4}\mathrm{CR}(\exp(\eta^{+}),0)\mathrm{CR}(\exp(-\eta^{-}),0)}}\right)^{-\frac{\alpha^{2}}{2}+Q\alpha-2}\,d\mathrm{LF}_{\mathcal{C}}^{(\alpha,\pm\infty)}.

Proof.

The proof is identical to that of Lemma
8.10
.
∎

Now, we give the analog of Lemma
8.11
. Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{2}^{\mathrm{f.l.}}(t,\ell)
denote the law of a sample from
\mathcal{M}_{2}^{\mathrm{f.l.}}(t,\ell)
with a marked point sampled from the probability measure proportional to generalized boundary length.

Lemma 8.33
.

There is a constant
C=C(\gamma)
such that the following holds.
Suppose
\alpha\in(\frac{\gamma}{2},Q)
.
Sample
(\phi,\eta,p^{-},p^{+})
from the measure

C\cdot\mathrm{LF}_{\mathcal{C}}^{(\alpha,\pm\infty)}(d\phi)\,\mathcal{L}_{\kappa^{\prime}}^{\alpha}(d\eta)\,\mathrm{Harm}_{-\infty,\eta^{-}}(dp^{-})\,\mathrm{Harm}_{+\infty,\eta^{+}}(dp^{+}),

and further sample a point
q\in\eta
from the probability measure proportional to generalized quantum length. Then the curve
\eta
cuts
(\mathcal{C},\phi,\eta,-\infty,+\infty,p^{-},p^{+},q)/{\sim_{\gamma}}
into a pair of forested quantum surfaces
\mathcal{D}^{-}
and
\mathcal{D}^{+}
(respectively containing marked points
(-\infty,p^{-},q)
and
(+\infty,p^{+},q)
) with the following description.

Embed the connected component of
\mathcal{D}^{\pm}
containing the bulk marked point as
(\mathbbm{H},X^{\pm},i,0)
(so
X^{\pm}=\phi\circ\psi_{\eta}^{\pm}+Q\log|(\psi_{\eta}^{\pm})^{\prime}|
), and let
\mathcal{L}^{\pm}_{\bullet}
be the forested line segment corresponding to the foresting of the boundary
\partial\mathbbm{H}
in counterclockwise direction starting from
0
, so
\mathcal{L}^{\pm}_{\bullet}
has a marked point on its forested boundary arc. Then the law of
(X^{-},\mathcal{L}^{-}_{\bullet},\mathcal{L}^{+}_{\bullet},X^{+})
is

\iiint_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{LF}_{\mathbbm{H}}^{(\alpha,i)}(t^{-})\times\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{2}^{\mathrm{f.l.}}(t^{-},\ell)\times\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{2}^{\mathrm{f.l.}}(t^{+},\ell)\times\mathrm{LF}_{\mathbbm{H}}^{(\alpha,i)}(t^{+})\,dt^{-}\,\cdot\ell d\ell\cdot\,dt^{+}.

(8.43)

Proof.

We first establish the
\alpha=\gamma
case.
Writing
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}^{\pm}
for
\mathcal{D}^{\pm}
without the marked point
q
, Lemma
8.31
identifies the law of (
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}^{-},\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}^{+}
) as
C\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\gamma;\ell)\times\mathcal{M}^{\rm f.d.}_{1,0,0}(\gamma;\ell)\ell d\ell
. Thus, if we let
\mathcal{L}^{\pm}
be
\mathcal{L}^{\pm}_{\bullet}
with marked point forgotten, by Lemma
2.21
and the definition of
\mathcal{M}_{1,0,0}^{\mathrm{f.d.}}(\gamma;\ell)
(Definition
2.32
) the law of
(X^{-},\mathcal{L}^{-},\mathcal{L}^{+},X^{+})
is (
8.43
) with the measures
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{2}^{\mathrm{f.l.}}(t^{\pm},\ell)
replaced by
\mathcal{M}_{2}^{\mathrm{f.l.}}(t^{\pm},\ell)
. The desired
\alpha=\gamma
result then holds by the uniform conformal welding claim in Lemma
8.31
.

The extension to the general
\alpha
case is identical to that in the proof of Lemma
8.11
, using Lemma
8.32
(in place of Lemma
8.10
) and Lemma
8.8
.
∎

.

Proof of Lemma
8.26
.

In Lemma
8.33
,
by Definition
2.32
the pair
(\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}^{-},\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}^{+})
agree in law with a sample from

\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;\ell)\times\mathcal{M}^{\rm f.d.}_{1,0,0}(\alpha;\ell)\ell d\ell
after a boundary point is sampled from the probability measure proportional to generalized boundary length for each surface. Thus, for
(\phi,\eta)\sim C\mathrm{LF}_{\mathcal{C}}^{(\alpha,\pm\infty)}\mathcal{L}_{\kappa^{\prime}}^{\alpha}
the law of
(\mathcal{C},\phi,\eta,-\infty,+\infty)/{\sim_{\gamma}}
is
\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{Weld}(\mathcal{M}_{1,0,0}^{\rm f.d.}(\alpha;\ell),\mathcal{M}^{\rm f.d.}_{1,0,0}(\alpha;\ell))\ell d\ell
. The desired result then follows from Proposition
8.3
.
∎

9
Evaluation of constants for the loop intensity measure

In this section, we evaluate the constants mentioned in Section
1.5
for the loop intensity measure.

9.1
The dilation constant

The first constant concerns the decomposition of the loop measure into its shape measure and a dilation. For convenience, we map the measure to the infinite cylinder
\mathcal{C}
so that dilation becomes translation. Let
\widetilde{\SLE}_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{sep}}
be the measure on the space of loops in
\mathcal{C}
separating
\pm\infty
which is obtained by sampling a loop from
\widetilde{\SLE}_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{loop}}
, mapping it to
\mathcal{C}
via
\log
, and restricting to
\{\text{loop separates }\pm\infty\}
. For a loop
\eta\sim\widetilde{\SLE}_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{sep}}
we define a pair
(\eta_{0},\textbf{t})
, where
\textbf{t}=\sup_{z\in\eta}\Re z
and
\eta_{0}=\eta-\textbf{t}
is the translation of
\eta
such that
\sup_{z\in\eta_{0}}\Re z=0
. On the other hand, given a pair
(\eta_{0},\textbf{t})
, we can recover
\eta=\eta_{0}+\textbf{t}
. Thus we may view
\widetilde{\SLE}_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{sep}}
as the law of the pair
(\eta_{0},\textbf{t})
.

Proposition 9.1
.

Let
\kappa\in(8/3,8)
. There is a probability measure
\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}
such that

\widetilde{\SLE}_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{sep}}=\frac{1}{\pi}{(\frac{\kappa}{4}-1)\cot(\pi(1-\frac{4}{\kappa}))}\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}(d\eta_{0})\,dt.

To prove Proposition
9.1
, we need the following asymptotic result.

Lemma 9.2
.

Let
\kappa\in(8/3,8)
and
C>0
. Sample a full-plane
\CLE_{\kappa}
and let
N_{C}\geq 0
be the number of loops
\eta
which separate
0
and
\infty
satisfying
e^{-C}\leq\mathrm{dist}(\eta,0)\leq 1
. Then

\lim_{C\to\infty}\frac{1}{C}\mathbbm{E}[N_{C}]=\frac{1}{\pi}(\frac{\kappa}{4}-1)\cot(\pi(1-\frac{4}{\kappa})).

(9.1)

Proof.

Let
\dots,\eta_{-1},\eta_{0},\eta_{1},\dots
be the doubly-infinite sequence of loops separating
0
and
\infty
such that each successive loop is nested in the previous loop, and
\eta_{0}
is the first loop which intersects
\mathbbm{D}
. Let
B_{n}:=\log\mathrm{CR}(\eta_{n-1},0)-\log\mathrm{CR}(\eta_{n},0)
, so the sequence
B_{1},B_{2},\dots
is i.i.d., and
[
SSW09
, Proposition 1, Equation (2)]
gives

\frac{1}{\mathbbm{E}[B_{1}]}=\frac{1}{\pi}{(\frac{\kappa}{4}-1)\cot(\pi(1-\frac{4}{\kappa}))}.

For
n\geq 0
let
S_{n}:=B_{1}+\dots+B_{n}
and let
R:=\mathrm{CR}(\eta_{0},0)
, so
\mathrm{CR}(\eta_{n},0)=e^{-S_{n}}R
. Let
\tau_{C}\geq 0
be the first index such that
S_{\tau_{C}}>C-\log 4+\log R
, and let
\sigma_{C}\geq 0
be the first index such that
S_{\sigma_{C}}>C+\log R
. Then by the Koebe quarter theorem

\mathrm{dist}(\eta_{\tau_{C}-1},0)\geq\frac{1}{4}\mathrm{CR}(\eta_{\tau_{C}-1},0)\geq e^{-C},\qquad\mathrm{dist}(\eta_{\sigma_{C}},0)\leq\mathrm{CR}(\eta_{\sigma_{C}},0)\leq e^{-C},

hence

\tau_{C}\leq N_{C}\leq\sigma_{C}+1.

(9.2)

We will show that
\lim_{C\to\infty}\mathbbm{E}[\tau_{C}]/C=\lim_{C\to\infty}\mathbbm{E}[\sigma_{C}]/C=1/\mathbbm{E}[B_{1}]
. This and (
9.2
) imply the desired (
9.1
).

First,
the explicit density for
B_{1}
computed in
[
SSW09
, (4)]
implies that for some constants
a,b>0
we have
\mathbbm{P}[B_{1}\in dx]=(1+o_{x}(1))ae^{-bx}
as
x\to\infty
, so there is a constant
K
such that for any
x>0
, we have
\mathbbm{E}[B_{1}\mid B_{1}>x]<x+K
. We conclude that
\mathbbm{E}[S_{\sigma_{C}}\mid R]\leq C+\log R+K
. By Wald’s identity, we have
\mathbbm{E}[\sigma_{C}\mid R]=\mathbbm{E}[S_{\sigma_{C}}\mid R]/\mathbbm{E}[B_{1}]\leq(C+\log R+K)/\mathbbm{E}[B_{1}]
. Since
R=\mathrm{CR}(\eta_{0},0)\leq 4
by the Koebe quarter theorem, we conclude
\limsup_{C\to\infty}\mathbbm{E}[S_{\sigma_{C}}]/C\leq 1/\mathbbm{E}[B_{1}]
.

Next, by Wald’s identity
\mathbbm{E}[\tau_{C}\mid R]=\mathbbm{E}[S_{\tau_{C}}\mid R]/\mathbbm{E}[B_{1}]\geq(C-\log 4+\log R)/\mathbbm{E}[B_{1}]
. Thus for any constant
x>0
we have
\mathbbm{E}[\tau_{C}]\geq\mathbbm{E}[\tau_{C}1_{R>x}]\geq\mathbbm{P}[R>x](C-\log 4+\log x)/\mathbbm{E}[B_{1}]
, and
\liminf_{C\to\infty}\mathbbm{E}[\tau_{C}]/C\geq\mathbbm{P}[R>x]/\mathbbm{E}[B_{1}]
. Since
x
is arbitrary we have
\liminf_{C\to\infty}\mathbbm{E}[\tau_{C}]/C\geq 1/\mathbbm{E}[B_{1}]
. Combining with the previous paragraph gives the desired result.
∎

Proof of Proposition
9.1
.

By (
9.1
) we have
\widetilde{\SLE}_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{sep}}[0\leq\textbf{t}\leq C]=(1+o(1))\frac{1}{\pi}{(\frac{\kappa}{4}-1)\cot(\pi(1-\frac{4}{\kappa}))}C
, so the conformal invariance of
\widetilde{\SLE}_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{loop}}
implies the marginal law of
\mathbf{t}
is
\frac{1}{\pi}{(\frac{\kappa}{4}-1)\cot(\pi(1-\frac{4}{\kappa}))}\,dt
. Conformal invariance also implies the conditional law of
\eta_{0}
given
\mathbf{t}
does not depend on
\mathbf{t}
; this conditional law is
\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}
.
∎

9.2
The welding constant for
\kappa\in(\frac{8}{3},4)

For
\kappa\in(\frac{8}{3},4)
and
\gamma=\sqrt{\kappa}
, Theorems
3.2
and
1.1
imply that

\mathrm{Weld}(\mathrm{QD},\mathrm{QD})=C_{\gamma}\mathrm{QS}\otimes\widetilde{\SLE}_{\kappa}^{\rm loop}

(9.3)

For some constant
C_{\gamma}
. We now evaluate this constant.

Proposition 9.3
.

For
\kappa\in(\frac{8}{3},4)
and
\gamma=\sqrt{\kappa}
, we have

C_{\gamma}=\frac{1}{4\pi}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4})\Gamma(1-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4})}{(Q-\gamma)^{2}}\tan(\pi(\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}}-1)),

(9.4)

As same in the proof of Proposition
6.5
, we sample two points from quantum area measure and restrict to the event that the loop separates the two points. Then it suffices to prove (we incur a factor of 2 since there are two choices for which quantum disk the first point lies on)

2\mathrm{Weld}(\mathrm{QD}_{1,0},\mathrm{QD}_{1,0})=C_{\gamma}\cdot\mathrm{QS}_{2}\otimes\widetilde{\SLE}_{\kappa}^{\rm sep}

with the same
C_{\gamma}
as (
9.4
). This can be done by the following lemma.

Lemma 9.4
.

We have
\mathrm{Weld}(\mathrm{QD}_{1,0},\mathrm{QD}_{1,0})=\widetilde{K}_{\gamma}\mathrm{QS}_{2}\otimes(\mathcal{L}_{\kappa}\times dt)
, with the welding constant

\widetilde{K}_{\gamma}=\frac{\gamma}{16\pi^{2}(Q-\gamma)}\Gamma(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4})\Gamma(1-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}).

Proof.

By Proposition
8.4
, there exists a constant
C
such that

\int_{0}^{\infty}\ell\mathrm{Weld}(\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\mathrm{disk}}(\gamma;\ell),\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\mathrm{disk}}(\gamma;\ell))\,d\ell=C(Q-\gamma)^{2}\mathcal{M}_{2}^{\mathrm{sph}}(\gamma)\otimes(\mathcal{L}\times dt).

We evaluate the event
E_{\delta,\varepsilon}
(i.e. the total quantum area is larger than 1 and the quantum length of the
\SLE_{\kappa}
loop is in
(\delta,\varepsilon)
; see the paragraph before Lemma
8.5
on both sides of the above welding equation).
By Lemma
8.5
and Proposition
8.6
, we have

\frac{1}{C(Q-\gamma)^{2}}\frac{1}{\frac{2}{\gamma}(Q-\gamma)\Gamma(\frac{2}{\gamma}(Q-\gamma))}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}2^{-\gamma^{2}/2}\overline{U}(\gamma)}}\right)^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(4\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4}}}\right)^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}(Q-\gamma)}=\frac{\overline{R}(\gamma)}{2(Q-\gamma)^{2}},

hence

C=\frac{2^{3-\gamma^{2}}}{\gamma^{2}\Gamma(\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}})}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(4\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4}}}\right)^{1-\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}}}\frac{\overline{U}(\gamma)^{2}}{\overline{R}(\gamma)}=\frac{Q-\gamma}{4\gamma}\Gamma(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4})\Gamma(1-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}).

Here, we are using the following computation which uses
\Gamma(x)\Gamma(1-x)=\frac{\pi}{\sin(\pi x)}
and
\Gamma(x+1)=x\Gamma(x)
:

\displaystyle\frac{\overline{U}(\gamma)^{2}}{\overline{R}(\gamma)}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left.\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2^{2-\gamma^{2}}\pi^{2}}{\Gamma(1-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4})}}}\right)^{\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}}-1}\Gamma(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4})^{2}}}\right/\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\pi\Gamma(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4})}{\Gamma(1-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4})^{2}}}}\right)^{\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}}-1}\frac{-\frac{\gamma}{2}\Gamma(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}-1)}{(Q-\gamma)\Gamma(1-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4})\Gamma(\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}}-1)}}}\right)

\displaystyle=2^{\gamma^{2}-4}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{4\pi}{\Gamma(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4})\Gamma(1-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4})}}}\right)^{\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}}-1}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4})^{2}}{-\frac{\gamma}{2}\Gamma(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}-1)}(Q-\gamma)\Gamma(1-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4})\Gamma(\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}}-1)

\displaystyle=2^{\gamma^{2}-5}\gamma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(4\sin(\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4})}}\right)^{\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}}-1}\Gamma(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4})(Q-\gamma)\Gamma(1-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4})\Gamma(\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}})

Also, by Theorem
2.22
, we have
\mathcal{M}_{1,0}^{\mathrm{disk}}(\gamma;\ell)=\frac{2\pi}{\gamma}(Q-\gamma)^{2}\mathrm{QD}_{1,0}(\ell)
. And by definition we have
\mathcal{M}_{2}^{\mathrm{sph}}(\gamma)=\mathrm{QS}_{2}
. This gives
\widetilde{K}_{\gamma}={C(Q-\gamma)^{2}}/{(\frac{2\pi}{\gamma}(Q-\gamma)^{2})^{2}}
as desired.
∎

Proof of Proposition
9.3
.

Combining Lemma
9.4
and Proposition
9.1
, the result follows.
∎

Another natural formulation of conformal welding result for the SLE loop measure is under the uniform embedding
\mathbf{m}_{\widehat{\mathbbm{C}}}
. For the loop intensity measure, we have

\mathbf{m}_{\widehat{\mathbbm{C}}}\ltimes\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\ell\mathrm{Weld}(\mathrm{QD}(\ell),\mathrm{QD}(\ell))\,d\ell}}\right)=K_{\gamma}\mathrm{LF}_{\mathbbm{C}}\times\widetilde{\SLE}_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{loop}}

(9.5)

where
K_{\gamma}
is a constant depends on
\gamma
. We now evaluate this constant as a corollary of Proposition
9.3
.

Corollary 9.5
.

For
\kappa\in(\frac{8}{3},4)
and
\gamma=\sqrt{\kappa}
, we have

K_{\gamma}=\frac{\gamma}{8}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4})\Gamma(1-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4})}{(Q-\gamma)^{4}}\tan(\pi(\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}}-1)).

Proof.

Applying the uniform embedding
\mathbf{m}_{\widehat{\mathbbm{C}}}
in Proposition
9.3
gives

\mathbf{m}_{\widehat{\mathbbm{C}}}\ltimes\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\ell\mathrm{Weld}(\mathrm{QD}(\ell),\mathrm{QD}(\ell))\,d\ell}}\right)=C_{\gamma}\mathbf{m}_{\widehat{\mathbbm{C}}}\ltimes\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathrm{QS}\times\widetilde{\SLE}_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{loop}}}}\right).

Since
\mathbf{m}_{\widehat{\mathbbm{C}}}\ltimes\mathrm{QS}=\frac{\pi\gamma}{2(Q-\gamma)^{2}}\mathrm{LF}_{\mathbbm{C}}
(Proposition
2.13
), we conclude that
K_{\gamma}=C_{\gamma}\frac{\pi\gamma}{2(Q-\gamma)^{2}}
. Plugging (
9.4
) yields the result.
∎

9.3
The welding constant for
\kappa^{\prime}\in(4,8)

We first prove the counterpart of Proposition
9.3
.

Proposition 9.6
.

For
\kappa^{\prime}\in(4,8)
and
\gamma=\frac{4}{\sqrt{\kappa^{\prime}}}
, we have
\mathrm{Weld}(\mathrm{GQD},\mathrm{GQD})=C_{\gamma}^{\prime}\mathrm{QS}\otimes\widetilde{\SLE}_{\kappa^{\prime}}^{\rm loop}
(by Theorems
3.3
and
1.1
) with

C_{\gamma}^{\prime}={R_{\gamma}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}}}\right)^{5}\pi^{-\frac{8}{\gamma^{2}}+2}2^{-\frac{8}{\gamma^{2}}}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}}-1)}{\Gamma(2-\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}})}\Gamma(1-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4})^{\frac{8}{\gamma^{2}}+2}\tan\pi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}-1}}\right)\frac{2(Q-\gamma)^{2}}{\pi\gamma},

(9.6)

where
R_{\gamma}^{\prime}
is specified by
|\mathrm{GQD}(\ell)|=R_{\gamma}^{\prime}\cdot\ell^{-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}-2}
as in Lemma
2.28
.

Similary as in the proof of Proposition
9.6
we need the following lemma.

Lemma 9.7
.

The constant
\widetilde{K}^{\prime}_{\gamma}
in the welding equation
\int_{0}^{\infty}\ell\mathrm{Weld}(\mathrm{GQD}_{1,0}(\ell),\mathrm{GQD}_{1,0}(\ell))d\ell=\widetilde{K}^{\prime}_{\gamma}\mathrm{QS}_{2}\times\mathcal{L}_{\kappa^{\prime}}(d\eta_{0})dt
satisfies

\widetilde{K}^{\prime}_{\gamma}\frac{\gamma^{2}\bar{R}(\gamma)}{8(Q-\gamma)^{2}}=\frac{{R_{\gamma}^{\prime}}^{2}\Gamma(4/\kappa^{\prime})}{\Gamma(2-4/\kappa^{\prime})\Gamma(2-\kappa^{\prime}/4)}(4\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4})^{-\kappa^{\prime}/4+1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{16\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4}}\frac{\Gamma(1-\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}})}{\Gamma(\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}})}}}\right)^{2}

Proof.

Recall
E_{\delta,\varepsilon}
is the event that the generalized quantum disk containing the first marked point has quantum area
t least 1 and the loop has boundary length in
(\varepsilon,\delta)
. We evaluate
E_{\delta,\varepsilon}
on both sides of the welding equation. By Proposition
8.29
and taking
\alpha=\gamma
, we have

(\mathrm{QS}_{2}\otimes(\mathcal{L}_{\kappa^{\prime}}\times dt))[E_{\delta,\varepsilon}]=(1+o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1))\frac{\gamma^{2}\bar{R}(\gamma)}{8(Q-\gamma)^{2}}\log\varepsilon^{-1},

(9.7)

while evaluating
E_{\delta,\varepsilon}
on the measure
\int_{0}^{\infty}\ell\mathrm{Weld}(\mathrm{GQD}_{1,0}(\ell),\mathrm{GQD}_{1,0}(\ell))d\ell
gives

\int_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\ell|\mathrm{GQD}_{1,0}(\ell)|^{2}\mathrm{GQD}_{1,0}(\ell)^{\#}[A>1]d\ell.

(9.8)

Note that
\mathrm{GQD}_{1,0}(\ell)^{\#}[A>1]=\mathrm{GQD}_{1}(1)^{\#}[A>\ell^{-8/\kappa^{\prime}}]
. According to Lemma
8.27
, we see

\mathrm{GQD}_{1}(1)^{\#}[A>\ell^{-8/\kappa^{\prime}}]=\frac{\Gamma(4/\kappa^{\prime})}{\Gamma(2-4/\kappa^{\prime})\Gamma(2-\kappa^{\prime}/4)}(4\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4})^{-\kappa^{\prime}/4+1}\ell^{2-8/\kappa^{\prime}}+O(\ell^{2}).

Then (
9.8
) equals

\displaystyle\int_{\varepsilon}^{\delta}\ell|\mathrm{GQD}_{1,0}(\ell)|^{2}\mathrm{GQD}_{1,0}(\ell)^{\#}[A>1]d\ell

(9.9)

\displaystyle=\int_{\varepsilon}^{\delta}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{\Gamma(4/\kappa^{\prime})}{\Gamma(2-4/\kappa^{\prime})\Gamma(2-\kappa^{\prime}/4)}(4\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4})^{-\kappa^{\prime}/4+1}\ell^{2-8/\kappa^{\prime}}+O(\ell^{2})}}\right]l\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R_{\gamma}^{\prime}\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{16\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4}}\frac{\Gamma(1-\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}})}{\Gamma(\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}})}\ell^{\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}}-2}}}\right)^{2}d\ell

\displaystyle=\frac{{R_{\gamma}^{\prime}}^{2}\Gamma(4/\kappa^{\prime})}{\Gamma(2-4/\kappa^{\prime})\Gamma(2-\kappa^{\prime}/4)}(4\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4})^{-\kappa^{\prime}/4+1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{16\sin\frac{\pi\gamma^{2}}{4}}\frac{\Gamma(1-\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}})}{\Gamma(\frac{4}{\kappa^{\prime}})}}}\right)^{2}(1+o_{\delta,\varepsilon}(1))\log\varepsilon^{-1}.

Let (
9.7
) equal the last line of (
9.9
), the result then follows.
∎

Proof of Proposition
9.6
.

Combining Lemma
9.7
and Proposition
9.1
, we get the result.
∎

As in Corollary
9.5
, in the
the uniform embedding setting we have:

Corollary 9.8
.

We have

\mathbf{m}_{\widehat{\mathbbm{C}}}\ltimes\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\ell\mathrm{Weld}(\mathrm{GQD}(\ell),\mathrm{GQD}(\ell))d\ell}}\right)=K^{\prime}_{\gamma}\mathrm{LF}_{\mathbbm{C}}\times\widetilde{\SLE}_{\kappa^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{loop}}

where the constant
K^{\prime}_{\gamma}
is given by

\frac{K^{\prime}_{\gamma}}{{R_{\gamma}^{\prime}}^{2}}=-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}}}\right)^{5}\pi^{-\frac{8}{\gamma^{2}}+2}2^{-\frac{8}{\gamma^{2}}}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}}-1)}{\Gamma(2-\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}})}\Gamma(1-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4})^{\frac{8}{\gamma^{2}}+2}\tan\pi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}-1}}\right)

(9.10)

Proof.

We apply the uniform embedding
\mathbf{m}_{\widehat{\mathbbm{C}}}
to Proposition
9.6
and use
\mathbf{m}_{\widehat{\mathbbm{C}}}\ltimes\mathrm{QS}=\frac{\pi\gamma}{2(Q-\gamma)^{2}}\mathrm{LF}_{\mathbbm{C}}
(Proposition
2.13
). Then we conclude
K^{\prime}_{\gamma}=C_{\gamma}^{\prime}\frac{\pi\gamma}{2(Q-\gamma)^{2}}
for
C_{\gamma}^{\prime}
in (
9.6
); and the result follows by plugging into the expression of the reflection coefficient
\bar{R}(\gamma)
in Lemma
2.8
.
∎

Appendix A
Continuity of
\CLE_{\kappa}
as
\kappa\uparrow 4

In this section we supply the continuity in
\kappa
needed to extend Theorems
1.1
and
1.3

from
\kappa<4
to
\kappa=4
. We start with a monotonicity statement for
\CLE_{\kappa}
proved in
[
SW12
]
.

Lemma A.1

(
[
SW12
]
)
.

There exists a coupling of
\CLE_{\kappa}
on the unit disk
\mathbbm{D}
for
\kappa\in(\frac{8}{3},4]
such that a.s. each outermost loop of
\CLE_{\kappa_{1}}
is surrounded by an outermost loop of
\CLE_{\kappa_{2}}
if
\kappa_{1}<\kappa_{2}\leq 4
.

Proof.

By
[
SW12
, Theorems 1.5, 1.6]
, the law of the boundaries of outermost loop clusters in a Brownian loop soup with intensity

c_{\kappa}=(3\kappa-8)(6-\kappa)/2\kappa
is given by the outermost loops of
\CLE_{\kappa}
. Now the monotonicity of
c_{\kappa}
in
\kappa\in(\frac{8}{3},4]

yields the desired monotonicity in Lemma
A.1
.
∎

We recall the formula from
[
SSW09
]
for the conformal radii of CLE.

Theorem A.2

(
[
SSW09
]
)
.

For
\kappa\in(8/3,8)
, let
\eta_{\kappa}
be the outermost loop surrounding
0
of a
\CLE_{\kappa}
on
\mathbbm{D}
. Let
\mathrm{CR}(\eta_{\kappa},0)
